ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e2742	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XX/M/M_00017/index.shtml -- 17/M]] What concrete measures have you taken to limit abuse in the field of health insurance?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e2770	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e2785	Ladies and gentlemen! In the field of health insurance, there is an institutionally sufficient amount of measures and organisational measures against abuse. I would just like to mention a few of these: In the field of control of the correct performance of the benefits, the first of all within the insurance companies, the insurance institution, of course, is to check the requirement of entitlement, for example, whether the family property is given or whether the insurance is given at all. It is also controlled during performance. There are, for example, controls on the provision of sickness benefit. There are also checks of the principal for certain services. In addition, there is control in the area of relations with the contracting parties, where the billing documents are examined. There are sample tests to determine whether the calculated benefits are actually provided. At the same time, according to statistical evaluations, the credibility of performance rates is also verified and further examination measures are also taken by sampling. Such tests are also carried out in the field of all other services. It is also crucial that, in the field of contribution services, the service providers of their own organisational units are examined whether the reporting obligation is met, whether the contribution payments are made. Within the insurance institutions there is also – and this is particularly important – a very good mechanism of control: from the internal revision to the control of the entire conduct by the control assembly, through the understanding of the institutions of the Federal Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs and ultimately through the Court of Auditors' conduct.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e2977	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e2992	In the case of increases in expenditure, the cause must first be addressed. There are two fundamental problems: Firstly, there are cost increases whose causes are positive: The people live happily longer, the medicine makes progress, there are constantly new methods of diagnosis and therapy. The resulting cost increases are therefore a positive cause. We want to continue to cover these in the future. In addition, there are cost increases that are avoidable, cost increases that can be prevented by better organisation through consistent negotiations with the contracting parties. With regard to this area there has been a number of negotiations in recent months with the main association and then from the main association with the pharmaceutical industry, with the pharmacy chamber, with the doctors and with the manufacturers of healing aids and aids. These have shown that in these areas we will achieve cost savings and cost reductions in the next few years. These will account for about 4 billion schillings in the area of the local health telephone next year. By the way, the cost gap has also increased further because, as a result of the weaker economic development, revenue increases are lower than in previous years. But, as has been said, two thirds of the cost developments that have led to a deficit are brought in by cost-effective measures.

[[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In many countries of Europe, youth unemployment is a major problem and has devastating consequences. Austria currently has the lowest youth unemployment rate. However, we must focus our efforts on giving employment to the young people who leave school this year. In this context, the labour market service, in line with the Ministry of Economic Affairs, has created new measures to facilitate the teaching of young people. These promotions are put in place by the labour market service in such a way that not only a teaching centre is created, but young people are then more likely to find employment throughout their life. I would like to briefly explain the principles of these promotions, which are set out by the labour market service for apprentices. First of all, there is the principle that the teacher is encouraged to use the apprentices in the direction that they will then have the opportunity, because of their special qualifications, to make working life easier, to continue training and to be more flexible. An example: Workers, girls in atypical women's professions – and these are almost all – are particularly encouraged into teaching. There will be promotions for every girl in a men's profession, if I may say so, who will start teaching and thus have better opportunities in life in the future, and every teacher will receive 4,000 s for a girl's apprenticeship. Another example: It will provide special support for those teachers who train apprentices in such a way that they have learned several occupations after completion of the teaching, except those of today's classic dual teachings, and will grant the teacher a grant of 4,000 s per month for this multiple training. This has the advantage that, after a multiple training, not only multiple occupations have been learned, but that teaching has already learned to be flexible, and that is a broad basis for subsequent further qualification measures. In this area of youth, a total of around 600 million schilling and in the area of apprentices around 300 million schilling are spent by the labour market service this year.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e7366	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! An essential aspect of this innovation, which is under discussion today, is to ensure the financing of the high quality of our health care. In contrast to constantly quoted reports, "Safering the financing of health insurance funds", it is not about health insurance, but about ensuring the quality of health care. Thankfully, we have a good system in Austria, but we also need to note that, with pleasure, the average life expectancy is constantly increasing, that at the same time the possibilities of medicine in diagnosis, in therapy, are getting bigger and, of course, more complex. It is therefore necessary that we recognize these facts, which are positive in themselves – the longer life expectancy, the better possibilities of medicine – and that we make medical progress accessible to everyone in the future, without regard to their incomes – that is crucial – and that we also ensure financial viability. We must ensure financial viability in particular by reducing costs where this is possible for insured persons without loss of quality. In recent months and weeks – long before the liberals have come here in this House with measures to reduce costs – already with measures in the field of medical costs – without loss of quality for the insured – in the area of the cost of medications, cures, aids, decisively cost-damaging. During the negotiations on this subject over the last few weeks and months, which we have conducted with the main association of social security institutions and also with the pharmaceutical industry, with the Chamber of Doctors, with the countries on the financing of hospitals, we have found ways of reducing costs without losing quality for the insured two thirds of the need to finance health care. Two thirds of the cost increases can be achieved by making significant savings in the administration of costs, by ensuring with the countries in the field of hospital financing over the next few years that there will no longer be any significant cost increases, but that the costs of the so-called KRAZAF hospitals will develop in the next few years, in such a way that they will only rise to the extent that they will increase the revenue from the insurance contributions. At the same time, the pharmaceutical industry, with the pharmaceutical chambers, has also been agreed with the doctors in these areas that there will also be decisive cost-damaging measures here. These are the measures on the cost reduction side. Two thirds come in through cost reductions. However, we still have to make contributions to ensure health care, to ensure that health care is constantly increasing in quality, on the revenue side, to a reasonable degree, of course. I'm going to explain here again why this is reasonable. In the context of these measures, we have solved a crucial problem: As a result of the financial situation in recent months, health insurance has also been required to have more and more in mind the duration of health care. In the area of the Wiener Landeskrankenkasse, for these reasons, the constitutional possibilities of sickness benefits had to be reduced from 52 to 26 weeks. This decision of the Wiener Landeskrankenkasse is still in the Social Ministry. If we had not re-regulated the financing of this novel, we would not have had to re-established the possibility of introducing measures in the law, even in the case of many cash offices, these reductions in the amount of sickness benefits would have been necessary. It is therefore crucial that, in the sense of quality assurance with this novel, we raise the legal right to health care from 26 to 52 weeks, and that it is still possible for the cash registers, as it is today, that in those cases where the need for social medicine is necessary, this will be given beyond these 52 weeks, that is to say up to 78 weeks. In other words, the decisive part of this innovation today is that we make the financial guarantee that our good health system will continue to be so financially financial in the future – with all the advances in medicine, with all the needs that are happily linked to the rise of our age – that everyone – regardless of income – will be as involved in medical progress in the future as well as in the future, and that in the field of sickness benefits, no longer by law as yet 26 weeks will be covered by law. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] I believe that this is an essential social policy step for the future. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] After the cost savings from, as has been said, two thirds of the financing needs, it was necessary for us to discuss in the coalition and with the social partners how the difference that remains can be brought in. You know my personal attitude from many discussions. I was originally of the opinion that we could make a reasonable solution with a moderate increase in contributions for all. There are, of course, also discussions on additional wage costs and and so on. I do not hide that there is this need for discussion. The result was a compromise, a compromise which, in my view, is quite acceptable. This compromise is that, on the one hand, there will be no increase in the contribution of the active staff, but that there will also be no participation in the costs of medical care which could have led to undue hardships. The compromise does not apply to the area of active staff in those areas in which there is already self-retaining, for example with officials or with railways. In the future, there will be this compromise in the area of the regional health insurance fund, which is intended for the collection of a health certificate fee of 50 S in this novel – but with significant exceptions: This health certificate fee shall not be charged for children. She will not be taken in the event of transfers to the specialist, because there could have been an incalculable burden. The health certificate fee is also not abolished in the case of more socially weak or chronically ill patients, who are also exempt from the prescription fee. She is also not taken up by pensioners. For pensioners in the field of the ASVG, however, it will be possible, as I hope, to increase the contribution rates by 0.25 percent. These are at a pension of 10 000 S 25 S. Wherever I talk to people, I have found understanding that – as medical progress is really getting too good for everyone, because we need more medical services, because life expectancy has just increased – we also need to put up a little more after all the cost savings. This understanding has been confirmed in many congregations. Again: I'm not happy to bother anyone. But this is not a reduction in pensions, as some claim, but it is a contribution to good health care and health care. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This health certificate fee of 50 S per quarter, which is withdrawn in the case of assets, is not abolished by pensioners, because they will have to pay a slightly higher contribution fee in the future. The increase of 0.25 per cent causes pensioners in the ASVG sector to have a contribution rate of 3.55 per cent instead of so far. This is still lower than that of the active workers of 3.95 percent and is, for example, below that of the officials of 3.95 percent, also in the pension, or of railway pensioners who currently have a pension contribution of 4.6 percent. It was inevitable that even in the field of pensioners a little more was contributed to health care. In addition, this new health insurance scheme brings in funds from the family compensation fund in a fair manner. Health insurance is currently 50 : 50 years old with the burden of weekly allowance – a measure which is also important for family policy. In the future, like the karenz money, a division of 70: 30 will take place here. This means that health insurance in the area of the regional health insurance fund is supplied annually about 800 to 850 million schillings, which are not to be brought in by contribution increases. The funds for the Family Allowance Fund are spent on the resources of the employers, which is also offset by compensation. In order to collect the health certificate fee, the law states that this fee is abolished by the service providers – this was also represented in the discussion by the service providers. We will, together with the main association and the service providers, find a regulation in the near future that is as unbureaucratic as possible. Again: It would have been more unbureaucratic to increase the contribution rates to a moderate degree. There were, however, various resistances. I consider this measure to be compatible for the workers concerned, because it is independent of how often you go to the doctor within a quarter, and regardless of what costs actually apply to the doctor. Therefore, this is by no means a restraint, because 50 S per quarter means 50 S for three months – no matter how often you go to a practical doctor in these three months, regardless of which transfers are made by the practical doctor and regardless of what costs actually apply to the practical doctor. One thing we certainly did not want: that there is any control effect associated with it. Mr Oellinger has stressed this explicitly. I can't imagine anyone visiting a doctor just because this fee of 16.60 S – converted to the month – is lifted. I have the greater concern about this fee with regard to the bureaucracy which is to be mitigated. . I think it was necessary to find funding opportunities in this compromise to ensure the quality of health care in the future in order to ensure that everyone can also participate in the progress of medicine, and especially because we do not want to reduce the health care for those who are socially weak to 26 weeks. 26 weeks is currently in the law; according to your decision, the right to at least 52 weeks will be enshrined in the law, and that is important and valuable in social policy. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I have said that we have put in place savings measures in the area of management. There will be a stopover, there will be measures similar to those in the public service. These measures will be continued consistently. In the field of social security in 1992 the study of an external rationalization and consulting company, the company Hausemann, was conducted. This study foresees that a number of measures already taken – I do not have to list them here again – will be reviewed this year and next year for their effectiveness and then further steps will be taken. We will therefore, in agreement with the main association and with the individual social security funds following this Hausemann study, whose measures will now have to be checked for their efficiency, use an external advisory institution to determine precisely: How can we continue to implement measures in the field of management without loss of quality for insured persons, which have cost-effective effects? However, I would like to say the following: Of the approximately 115 to 118 billion Schilling, which are spent in the field of health insurance for insured persons, only 4 billion Schilling are in the field of administration. Administration means physical expenditure and personnel expenditure, including Porto and and so on. Whoever therefore believes that one can only achieve by means of administrative measures that will ensure the financing of health care in the future, without other measures, please consider this relation. Nevertheless: In the area of administration, measures are also being taken consistently. To this end, I would like to say to Mr Ollinger: I regret that it has obviously not been properly read what this control body is supposed to do in the main association. It is in no way about the fact that the main association should receive new competences at the expense of the individual health insurance institutions. However, Mr Oellinger: There is no contrast between the main association on the one hand and self-management on the other. The main association, like the individual insurance companies, is an instrument of self-management, yes, the main association is not just an instrument of self-management: The competent and responsible decision-makers in the main association are at the same time the owners and officials in the area of individual insurance companies. They are jointly represented in the main association due to the necessary synergies, they make decisions there, and it is right and good that the social security companies join together. Such a merger in the main association has happened. It will be all the more necessary in the future for these synergies to be used, because in all the negotiations with the contracting partners and the medical board – up to the field of hospital reform, up to the area of medication – efforts must be made to pursue income-oriented insurance policies, without losing quality for the insured. Therefore, the use of this synergy effect will be all the more necessary. This interaction is now being increased operationally. The main association does not receive any new skills – it has many competences – but the main association and the individual social security institutions will increasingly use the means and methods used today in the economy and will be good and reasonable. Therefore, I would like to ask you to read again from the Greens about your request, what is stated in this novel: a supplement to § 31 and in § 31 a part of the competences of the main association is anchored. The main association has, among other things, the competence to conclude the overall contracts. Any health insurance, which negotiates contracts with its national medical chambers, shall proceed with these contracts to the respective official in the main association, who shall vote there for total Austria, whether this contract is in order. It will become increasingly necessary to carry this overall responsibility more and more. § 31 states, inter alia, that the main association has the constant observation of the development of social security in its relations with the economy and the preparation of concrete proposals or the implementation of measures to maintain the continuing performance of social security without overburdening the economy. Again: The main association has the task of implementing measures to maintain the continuing performance of social security without overburdening the economy. And now we write about it – not as a new competence –: Hiezu, the main association has to set up an cross-insurance controlling with forward-looking and ongoing consideration of revenue and expenditure development. Whoever really reads this and is against these measures, I cannot understand it. [[Abg. Dr. Pumberger: I hope you read it yourself!]] And I am therefore convinced that if I have had the necessary additional discussions with the individual health insurance companies, they will very much agree with this measure, because it will secure a revenue-oriented contract policy in the future, with the means and methods of modern management technology. That's the point. And a goal will also be quality assurance for the insured without overburdening the economy, without taking into account the income situation. I therefore expect, Mr Oellinger, that you will withdraw your supplementary request in this direction, and I would like to explain this even more to you. – Thank you for your attention.

It has been possible for bakers to work at night, but for unskilled workers in this field, the possibility of night work remains dependent on collective agreement, on company agreements or individual agreements, on the operational or personal necessity and on the possibility of secure achievement of the holding or the availability of the way to operation. I think the bakers don't care if they live near the farm, whether they stay away or whether they can safely reach the farm at night. That's not where the question arises. In the case of the unearned, there is a need to re-establish business agreements which can remove the ban on night work. For us it would have been better, Minister, if there had been a general absence of the ban on night work for women and not just for a particular clique – I say so once, because that is what we can call. Minister! You know, we have been calling for a gender-neutral arrangement of night work for years. They also know that by 2001 the EU has an obligation to implement this scheme. Last year – and I can remember this Committee on Social Affairs very carefully – you were rather negative in our intentions regarding the abolition of the night work ban on women or a gender-neutral arrangement of night work. Now you have had to resist the pressure of a professional group, but unfortunately only to a limited extent and insufficient for all women working in this field. This change is too far-reaching to us. It would have been really better, as I have already mentioned, to abolish the ban on night work for all workers in this profession. One more word on the contract of work. I have already warned in the debate on the Structural Adjustment Act and at the time when this work contract regulation was adopted that there are about 200 000 to 300 000 women who will make it particularly when social security obligations are introduced in works contracts. I would like to make it quite clear to those women who are engaged in the so-called party distribution. At the time, I have reached my addresses from several large companies that make sales of between 4 and 5 billion schilling annually in Austria and which have said: In the event that this contractual arrangement enters into force at this low level, they will convert their sales, namely that they will go to neighbouring countries and carry out these orders by catalogue. – This would mean 200 000 to 300 000 women workers in this field, both in administration and in distribution, and also in production, job losses. They have now made a slight change because of this chaotic contract, but they have forgotten to raise the limit. Those workers and those women who urgently need a small sandwich for their families will now stand on the streets. Also those women, Mr Minister, who have no other possibilities because they do not find any other job due to the infrastructure, will lose this job, because the companies that wrote to me at that time have already partially withdrawn the consequences and will partly still attract them in the future. If you make a change to the contract of work, as has been said today – we try it out once – then, Minister, it will be too late, and the women are again the disadvantaged!

Since the 1980s, out-of-area operations have been discussed at the level of the European Union. These are the uses that take place in other countries outside Europe – they are now also decided – to safeguard and safeguard the interests of Europe when it comes to securing raw materials. This has nothing to do with defence, it has nothing to do with peace at all, these are classical interventions. The question is therefore: What did you think changed? If you are talking about a threat scenario and if you are talking about the fact that this threat scenario has changed, we are talking about what and where the threats of Europe are today. These "threats" – under quotations – that we have today, these are crises at social, economic and democratic level, which certainly have some countries of the former East. But the question is whether it is right to answer it with weapons, with a military alliance, with an intervention force. What do your threat scenarios look like when it comes to Easter expansion? What do your threat scenarios look like when NATO extends to the Russian border today? Is Russia really the threat scenario for you? Or where does this NATO limit stop that you can say that the security is now manufactured? Or how do you define the threat? You're not going into all of this. ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e11387	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Even if you, ladies and gentlemen, are instigating my statement, I must repeat it: We have been faced with completely new geopolitical conditions since 1989 and, in this context, Austria must constantly develop its security policy orientation. But not only Austria, but also the European Union has to face these new challenges in the context of the Intergovernmental Conference. As a member of the European Union, Austria is now taking the opportunity to take part in the development of European security architecture. In the sense of our comprehensive security concept, as outlined in our principle paper of the Austrian Federal Government for the Intergovernmental Conference, our accession to the EU was first and foremost the most important prerequisite for the security of Austria in Europe. Otherwise, we could not participate equally in any of these mechanisms. That is why the enlargement of the European Union, for example by the Viségrad countries and Slovenia, and at a later date also by others or by the Baltic States, is also an investment in the security of Europe, therefore also in ours. Such a comprehensive security concept – I stress this time and again – contains much more than traditional military security. The need of the Austrians for security – I think you can say that quietly – is hardly determined by a military threat. These are other factors to which we must devote our attention as much as the Union itself. What could endanger the security of each one of us is, for example, the internationally organised crime, which is the threat of unemployment, which we must also fight at European level, these are ethnic and social conflicts – I very much agree with what you have said – which can lead to political instability. There are also ecological threats which do not stop at borders. All these are very different forms of uncertainty, and it is therefore so essential that the European Union exploits all its possibilities for action: in the context of foreign policy, internal policy, judicial policy, economic policy, development policy and so on. The emergence of these threats, these risks must be combated at their social, economic and political roots. We Austrians have therefore made concrete proposals at European level, especially for the Intergovernmental Conference, to combat unemployment, to fight cross-border crime more effectively, or to strengthen environmental protection. Unemployment, we know, is ultimately just as a security problem as it may be a military threat. And if this is slowly recognized in Europe, not least because we have made a significant contribution to this education of opinion and knowledge. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I therefore believe that the European Union must devote itself to this wide range of security problems through the coordinated use of a variety of instruments. I also believe that a debate on security policy, which is reduced only to the question of joining a military alliance, is missing. Therefore, let us not reduce the necessary discussion on Austria's security to the question of membership of NATO, because it would not depress or depress at all many fears that concern the Austrians today. Unemployment, pollution, social insecurity are not solved by a military alliance, but by international cooperation that goes far beyond that. It is therefore important to come to a common European understanding of foreign and security policy challenges at this Intergovernmental Conference – because there are different opinions there too. Austria has been actively and in solidarity with the maintenance of world peace and international security for decades when it was called. I recall the commitment of the United Nations and OSCE. We have also not been afraid to participate in the WEU peacekeeping actions under the Common Foreign and Security Policy, although we have observer status and not member status. It is only today that we in the Federal Government have decided to assume our responsibility in the Mostar region, for example, and have extended the use of Austrian police officers in Mostar as a means of maintaining peace. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In this sense, we have also participated by sending a unit to IFOR to Bosnia. And that's where you asked: Why do we not think about our strengths by acting primarily in civil use? I believe that this is really our strength, but only this commitment is not sufficient. That is why we have understood a three-part approach to the problem: first of all: Second, participation in IFOR to participate in military security: Construction of civil structures, infrastructure, schools, hospitals et cetera, third: Repatriation of refugees in Austria. These are three elements to achieve one and the same goal. Because if military security is not given, then one cannot talk about economic reconstruction. And if there is no question of economic reconstruction, the return of refugees will not be achieved. Therefore, the three elements that are meaningfully interfering. Ladies and gentlemen! Furthermore, we have just chosen an activity there – you have referred to it as a truck driver or something similar, so not particularly qualified – in which Austria was able to bring its previous experience in the Peace Keeping. They also said that we would rather participate in supporting the holding of the elections in Bosnia-Herzegovina. I can tell you that from the Eastern Aid budget chapter, we are not making such insignificant amounts available to help the OSCE to ensure that these elections can be held in accordance with democratic rules. That's 10 million schilling equivalent in benefits and 3 million schilling in cash. Ladies and gentlemen! All this shows that we have no fear of contact, but that we are examining participation in international action on the basis of the law in force and depending on the objective of the operation in question. We do not press ahead with anything, but we are always accountable for what safety there and what our security serves. That's what we've done very well in recent history. We have been working at the Intergovernmental Conference – because you have also mentioned this – from the outset to ensure that the Union is given the opportunity to decide on humanitarian tasks and rescue operations, peacekeeping tasks and crisis management tasks. It is also consistent, I believe, that the Union must subsequently be able to give the WEU the mandate to carry out operations in this clearly defined framework. If you have mentioned the Petersberger tasks here, you are right with your list of all that the Petersberger tasks include. We have expressed on the part of the Austrian Federal Government, in which parts of this list – not in all, but in which parts – we can imagine ourselves to participate. That is not to say that we are concealing the others, but we have emphasized what we are participating in. I believe that this attitude, this Austrian provocation, to do so, is in line with our security policy tradition, which, moreover, is based on an active understanding of neutrality and corresponds to the changed security policy landscape. Ladies and gentlemen! I therefore think that we should be glad that the European Community is increasingly sharing this understanding which we are bringing into this process, namely to fight crises at the root and to see, in the prevention of conflict, in the prevention of the emergence of crises at all, the central challenge to dealing with crises in advance. Therefore, all allegations are not applicable that Austria is a safety-political driver. Within the outlines for a European security architecture, I consider the role of Austria to be a co-designer, a part-engineer of a total of peace and stability, and I believe that we must continue this path together. Question 4: As I have explained, I do not consider the upgrade to be an appropriate means of promoting security and stability. You are perfectly right if you point to possible social tensions if these economic foundations are not given. Furthermore, I do not think it appropriate to interfere in the internal affairs of other countries. It is precisely the decision on the security policy orientation of states that takes the core of sovereignty and is therefore to be decided by each state itself. Question 5: Madam Member, even if I wanted to make such an estimate of costs – which I do not want at all – this would not be possible because of the dependence on a large number of factors which are not yet finally to be assessed. To your questions 7 and 8: At the Intergovernmental Conference, Austria has always linked its proposal to proceed to majority decisions with the condition – I ask for attention, that is very important! – that there must at least be unanimity in the field of military security. This is in line with the Austrian position of principle, which I have set out several times here in the House. Moreover, this view was not disputed by the other Member States in the Intergovernmental Conference either. Ladies and gentlemen! In the Austrian position of principle for participation in the Intergovernmental Conference, it is said in this context – I quote, because it is really very important, two passages –: In order to improve the efficiency of the common foreign and security policy, Austria supports a gradual transition to majority decisions. In the light of experience in the first pillar, it can be expected that in practice voting would be the exception. However, the possibility of majority decisions alone would contribute to greater consensus and compromise and thus stimulate the decision-making process. However, in the following paragraph, it was necessary to take into account the specificities of CFSP decisions concerning partly sensitive aspects of state sovereignty in the transition to the majority principle. In particular, Austria considers that the area of military security must continue to be subject to unanimity in the future. In order to illustrate this, I have the document of the Permanent Substitute in Brussels. This document was created on date 12. It is therefore very up-to-date on June 1996. Under the heading "Clarification of the legal instruments of the Common Foreign and Security Policy", the sub-chapter "Decision Procedure" states: It is agreed that military security will continue to be decided unanimously in the future. We will also insist, because of our position in principle, that the principle laid down in Article J 4.4 of the Maastricht Treaty will continue to be maintained in the future, according to which the Union's security policy does not affect the particular nature of the Member States' security and defence policies – a very important aspect. Furthermore, I refer to my introductory basic remarks. – Thank you for your attention.

That's a contradiction! It is not about the principle of non-interference or interference, but about making politics! ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e11641	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I don't think it's good for Austria to keep talking about neutrality. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] No matter what it happens – in the will to abolish it, or, as today, by the request of the Greens, in the attempt to protect it –: That's why she gets into the talk. [[Ironic hustle and bustle with the Greens.]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: You are such a discussive party!]] In this sense, the green question has certainly not been urgent. She was intrusive, but she's really not urgent. [[Abg. Ing. Langthaler: So you want a secrecy?]] No, it's not about secrecy. It's not a secret. It is completely open and completely clear: We have a valid law. The Austrian neutrality exists, it has been further developed in terms of content, and this further development has also been a reference to those points which have been in the foreground of the negotiations since 1953 and in the parliamentary debate in 1955, namely the rejection of an alliance and the rejection of foreign troops in our territory. All the debates that are being held by you, by you constantly saying: She's in danger! She's in danger! Or from the other side, who says: Neutrality is abolished because it is a relic of the Cold War, it is absurd when it is said at the same time: But NATO should not be abolished and further developed, although it is also a relic of the cold war. It's clear: Neutrality has benefited us a lot, it continues to benefit us. And it is also clear that it was not an obstacle in the modern era, it was not an obstacle to us joining the EU, it does not prevent us from participating in the construction of a European security structure, it does not hinder us from participating in the resolution, nor does it prevent us from acting in solidarity: not just taking, but also giving, as some say. This will also be underlined by the Entrepreneurship Act, which makes it clear that Austria will participate in solidarity where these are international measures in accordance with our contractual obligations in the UN, the EU and the OSCE. The question of neutrality therefore does not arise, and the question of referendum is no more than the question of the abolition of neutrality. [[Abg. Dr. Ofner: He's right!]] It is theoretically clear by constitutional law: A departure from the status of persistent neutrality is not a total revision according to type. 44 para. 3 B-VG, and already in the explanatory remarks on the government template to the Neutrality Act, it was stated that neutrality does not constitute a building law. Constitutional voting is therefore not necessary; whether it is politically desirable will have to be decided by the far-off legislator, who may perhaps abolish it. If that happens, I don't know. [[Abg. May. Stadler: This is when the SPÖ is in opposition!]] A pre-emptive binding of the legislator is neither meaningful nor effective, and that is why our group will vote against the motion for a resolution. On the question raised by your foreign-political speaker in the explanatory statement, how this security architecture of Europe can now look out, we will certainly hear here again in the following contributions – especially the FPÖ, but perhaps also the Liberal Forum – how to develop existing institutions. I am always thinking that the military experts are actually not in agreement when the end of the cavalry is to come. Was it Friedland, or was it Borodino, or was it in the battle for Beersheba in 1917 – or perhaps only in 1945 in the Joglland? [[Abg. Haigermoser: 1970/71!]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: When the last Lipizzan dies! Then the cavalry is dead!]] The first mistake that cavalry can make is that the cavalry itself has survived, namely, the mistake of cutting the horse from the wrong side. This error is very often reflected in the military discussions, in the debates on military security. One merely assumes existing structures that one extrapolates, and does not ask: What can be? What could be? And: What is it? Ladies and gentlemen! Any debate on security must be based on clear objectives: What is Austria doing? What is important for Austria? What is important for Europe? In Europe, how do we create a system of comprehensive external and internal security that provides protection in the face of threats and attacks, which constitutes common measures that have a legal right to help and manufacture the rightful state, a corresponding network of early warning and supply facilities? It also raises the question: How do we respond to all the threats which are not military and which are much more dangerous to our security - the Chancellor has already spoken about this - and what do we do that there are mechanisms for peaceful conflict settlement between the participants of a security system and a contractually determined non-violence? We believe that it would be best to establish such a system from the head, i.e. from a state conference with a Treaty, possibly involving it in European law, and then to establish mechanisms and instruments for implementation based on these contractual rights. There is another tendency, namely the tendency to start from the other side: to approach a system of security from the existing institutions, from the alliance system and from the functioning military systems. There are some who do not even want to stay in existence. But it can be seen clearly, and it is reflected in the partnership for peace, in the reflections on NATO enlargement, in the Petersberg Declaration and in the Berlin proposals: This is clearly in the direction of slowly expanding and developing the military alliance system in terms of content by extending, extending and amending it. It would be hoped that this would lead to a safety system running smoothly. The NATO members are of course in favour of the latter procedure, even if they are in favour of a security system, because they are on familiar terrain, they have learned to deal with structures and hierarchies, it is the share over the Atlantic, the distribution of financing is clarity, and it is also in the interest of the arms industry and its suppliers companies that have fixed themselves on existing systems and who know that they have a certain guarantee. [[Abg. Scheibner: What are the NATO states: evil darkness?]] The administration that naturally wants to survive is also for this system. However, the main argument of the States is: There are functioning facilities that are in play and ready to use. The disadvantages of connection to the existing one are also clear: It's a very slow development. It is a one-sidedness, and there are many unresolved issues, such as security guarantees between members. We see that in NATO, although the issue of conflicts between Turkey and Greece is being put on ice, it is not really solved. There is the question of nuclear weapons, which has rightly been brought to the attention. There is also the question of the excolonial interests of individual members, and there are certainly also the psychological barriers to enlargement, namely that members of the former other side must join a changed relic from the cold war. [[Abg. Scheibner: They all want to go in!]] At the OSCE conference a few days ago, these lines of break were clearly shown: The Eastern European countries want Western countries to have NATO take the leading role in the OSCE, and the successor countries of the Soviet Union have advocated a European security system that is more than a changing NATO. [[Abg. Scheibner: Nevertheless, they all want to enter NATO: the Baltic and so on!]] They run against doors that are not as locked as they believe. Then: I believe that Austria would do well to seek, as the first possible solution, a security system with a comprehensive contract and, with a certain sense of reality, not to exclude the second solution. Of course, what is developing remains to be seen. It's not ready yet. But one day it may be that the existing European defence structures are developing a structure in which the elements of a European security system are quantitatively and qualitatively superior to the relics of a military alliance. That would be the time to ask the question of Austrian membership. To prevent all errors and misinterpretations: It is really not yet in place, and it would truly be premature to make a decision or statement in this regard today. Of course, some insist on a decision referring to the necessary security guarantees. I mean, Austria should not link participation in security systems with security guarantees which it does not need at all. In any event, Austria has proved itself sufficiently in the past and in the future is able to provide constructive contributions to international security without constantly talking about NATO accession and the abolition of neutrality on the one hand or, on the other hand, overcoming NATO and mystifying neutrality, as some have done. [[Abg. Wabl: What is mystified?]] I know that we are living at a time when we love gripping, title-like formulations, in which we want to have a yes or a no, but we all know that at the same time we are living in a world where the solutions are not just headline-like, not just a yes or a no, and that is why it is good for us to stick to our attitude: to look at the special security interests of Austria and to take a flexible position on the question of military structures. And a flexible position means waiting and then judging, does not mean opportunism, but guarantees that the necessary and politically meaningful can be actually made and thus a maximum of security is possible.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e11833	Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to express my awareness of the issue of neutrality in a special effort to get the dialogue here. I think it is an important topic for the future of our country, and it was an important topic in the past from which we should learn. I am therefore talking about dialogue because I was very disappointed by the statements made by Mrs Kammerlander. I'll deal with it later. Once the finding has been made: Neutrality is the best protection of our independence, our sovereignty. This is challenged as a result of accession to the EU. I would like to repeat: The main difference between the opinion of the requesting group, the Green Members, and that of the governmental parties is the question: What do we want? For us neutrality is a means. It was a means of achieving the independence of our country to restore freedom, to end the occupation and to ensure a future in democracy and independence. That was the goal of neutrality. The law therefore states very clearly: For the sake of the continued claim of its independence externally and for the purpose of the indestructibility of its territory, Austria shall declare its constant neutrality free of charge. "For the purpose of this" it is stated in the law. The purpose has been defined in the law at that time, which is very rare, ladies and gentlemen. ==References====External links== In October 1955, the motives and purposes of the Declaration of Neutrality are so clearly stated, because it is a very important act for the international position of Austria. He knew what he was doing. We are not concerned with neutrality, we are concerned with the security of this country. If this is secured by neutrality, it is good. That was the case back then. We were able to take the biggest step through the declaration of constant neutrality. If today we can guarantee the security of our country through association with other free countries, I think this is the best choice. I have to guarantee or ensure that the state can develop freely and in a democratic way. What I accomplish is secondary. It is the choice for which I have the responsibility in politics. That's the difference! [[In the case of the ÖVP and the Abg. May. Peter.]] They want neutrality on and on. What does this "to and for itself" mean? She's not a guarantee. It can only bring something in a certain situation. That's what we've practiced. Let us remember what neutrality was like in Belgium and in Luxembourg. She didn't use anything. She's not a guarantee. There's no such thing as that! The flexibility of neutrality is also occasionally an advantage, but shows how indispensable the legal structures in the international field are. That we lose credibility because we practice it flexibly is a self-critical way that is not justified. I should like to remind you that, for example, Switzerland has maintained its neutrality through practices that were more than problematic. Switzerland, for example, has let the officers of the Austro-Hungarian army into Graubünden when, because of the Risorgimento, it was able to assume that there might be an attack of Italy against Switzerland, because it has seen the Tessin in the maps of the Risorgimento representatives. In this case, one wanted to assure a powerful ally. They went to St. Moritz, on the border lines of the Italian-Swiss border, could see Italian positions to know that Austria-Hungary, if an attack on the Tessin, could assist Switzerland. This was, of course, a smooth break of neutrality. Officers were also arrested. Not much happened to them. They have been formally condemned, have looked forward to the end of the war in a nice country house. But Switzerland has made use of resources that were extremely worrying. Nobody claimed that Switzerland had not practised neutrality. What did Sweden do? – Sweden has always practised a neutrality that has found a great echo. The Swedish have concluded contracts with Great Germany which aircraft with which to land on the way to Russia, to Norway at airports. I do not show this because I condemn the countries. I regret that this was necessary. The responsibility for the state lay with the German state, was with the National Socialism. But they didn't comply with the rule of law because it was about their existence. This has never been done by Austria. We have always respected the two essential points of neutrality, namely that, firstly, no foreign troops are permanently stationed in Austria and that we do not join a classical military alliance. We do not need to be told that our neutrality is not credible. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Switzerland – and many know that I am a great admirer of Swiss foreign policy; saving its people two world wars is an achievement that is quite extraordinary and that can never be emphasized historically enough – practises a combination of realism, human perspectives and a commitment to national defense. It is not about, Mr Kammerlander, what we wish for, but about what is necessary in this world in order to ensure democracy and the freedom of people. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] You've been dealing with NATO. Arbitrator: Active Peace Policy, Peace Research!]] What does a serious international neutrality mean? – That is to say, one organizes his security and defence policy alone and isolated from other countries. I am not talking about overflight permits and transit permits of weapons and other equipment, but I am only talking about neutrality policy, Mr Arbitrary, which is also a condition for a neutral state in peace periods, so that we can say that in the future we will be able to stand out from all possible conflicts in the near future, of course, we will not join any organization which provides for collective defence measures. Mr. Schieder! That would be the conditions for an ever-neutral state. Switzerland, for example, took it very seriously. She said that we are staying out of all these security systems: no UN membership, no EU membership et cetera et cetera. Mr. Schieder! You, but also your Clubobmann Kostelka, have also repeatedly put the costs of NATO entry into play. However, one must also insist that one must organize and finance one's own defense. Mr. Schieder! [[Abg. Arbitrators: Do you believe in spirit voices? I didn't mention this with a word!]] What would it cost if we said that we had to upgrade our federal army so far that we could stand up to all possible potential threats to the future on our own? [[Abg. Dr. Cap: Do you really hear ghost voices?]] That's where you need to see the alternatives. [[Complaints with the liberals.]] The alternatives are not the same as before or join NATO, but the alternatives are either to organize security together with other countries or to upgrade themselves on their own in such a way that the deterrence potential can also be created against all possible aggressors. [[Abg. Dr. Cap: Why do you hear voices?]] Mr Cap! From you always comes the call: What are the threats? Then the Federal Chancellor comes together with the Greens and says that it is actually about social issues, about environmental issues. Ladies and gentlemen! As a responsible politician in Austria, we need to prepare ourselves for the case, which is not currently available, but is not excluded, as in the case of insurance. Now that we are at peace, this must be something of value to us, and we must also overcome past dogmas. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] We believe, ladies and gentlemen, that NATO and participation in common security systems have nothing to do with cold war instruments and military aggression, but that it is simply the command of the hour that we, together with the other Western states, create solidarity in Europe and, together with the other states, contribute to our defence. Nor can they pretend that what is going to develop in Europe is not yet foreseeable. The course has been set for a long time. The States of the European Community, the states of NATO, the states of the Western European Union have already noted that nothing new will develop in Europe, that one will not build a new structure, but that the existing institutions will be further developed, that they will be extended and that a security architecture will be built from these existing institutions. The Federal Chancellor has said that we are in the process of shaping it. You can't explain that to me. It is also purely optical for us to see how the situation is going, Mr Bundeskanzler! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] That is why this would be a command of the hour. Come on. Not now with the arguments of nuclear weapons and the stationing of foreign troops. Ladies and gentlemen! Federal Chancellor! Austria is not a driver of the security policy, because this does not allow the other states to have the benefits, without having to bear the obligations of membership. But Austria is becoming a spirit driver in European security policy. We are heading in the wrong direction and apparently do not know in which direction the security debate is going. Take the courage, finally put together with us the initiatives, put the membership proposals to NATO, to the Western European Union, create the conditions for a successful national defence, because then Austria's security for the future is assured! I think it is wrong to measure the contribution to stability in Europe primarily to accession or non-accession to a military alliance. What counts, ladies and gentlemen, is the willingness to participate actively in an international and European security cooperation. Our international solidarity certainly does not fail in neutrality! Even without membership of a military alliance and without unilateral partying, we are making a really important contribution to ensuring peaceful conditions in a controversial region. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The international peace efforts are no longer focused on the prevention of intergovernmental wars or the defence of attacks on the territory of sovereign states or states communities, such as the EU Territory, but on the prevention of conflicts within states or also in cross-border areas. These fundamental changes, of course, also require a new strategy in security policy, which goes far beyond the limited framework of a purely military alliance and can therefore not be exhausted only in a discussion of neutrality, NATO or WEU accession. Austria will participate in a future EU security system insofar as it is the fight against conflicts within the EU or the defence against external attacks on EU territory. However, we should clearly refuse to participate in military actions without cover through decisions by the United Nations or the OSCE or even in nuclear defence. Our aim must be to find comprehensive solutions to our security, far beyond the military sphere, including, of course, the economic, social and environmental dimension. Of course, Europe-wide cooperation is necessary and necessary, and we will be actively involved in it.

Another piece of lighting on NATO's legitimacy: At the Berlin Summit, not only were the out-of-area operations decided, which also enable the European NATO countries to carry out such missions alone, but it was established that, in addition to safeguarding the Treaty area, important tasks for these operations are the safeguarding of raw materials and free access to world markets. If nothing else, then this fact clearly and clearly underlines that it is not a peacekeeping force, a peace order, not even a defence alliance, but that it is a military pact with an intervention order when access to the raw materials and access to the world market is at risk to the European Union. Dr. Maitz: Without military protection, the whole other thing doesn't make sense!]] What we are proposing is to increase the OSCE mission with 100 million schillings and 200 experts from Austria in order to be able to carry out the return to refugees and the preparation of elections, and to do so on aid for reconstruction and economic aid instead of 90 with 190 million shillings in order to actually rebuild civilian life. This is what we consider to be the future challenges facing us in Europe. This will be the challenges not only in Bosnia, in ex-Yugoslavia, but unfortunately in many regions of Europe, especially in Eastern Europe. It is not only a question of using logistics, not only political will, but also financial resources where civil life can be protected preventively or rebuilt after military conflicts. My colleague, Mr Haselsteiner, said it very much, but I would nevertheless like to repeat it: If we don't care about the location quality, we will always have similar problems. And the quality of the location means that companies of this size and these capital requirements do not have any migration trends. It is not a moral event to lead a large group. That's what Hazelsteiner wanted to say: That one cannot rely on anyone to do something out of sympathy or affection if we do not either have a proper, proper contractual basis in our hands – that has rightly been agreed – or are a favourable location, so that we are invested for our own benefit, but for our benefit here. We have a problem of tension in relation to the East Reform States, and there is now this wage delta. It would be quite naïve to assume that only because we do not like the cost of wages in the comparable countries, or in the countries with which we are competing, will we be able to jump forward. Of course, in the long run, it is likely that wage levels will be adjusted, but – and here I wish to expressly contradict the colleague of the Socialist Group – it is not in the form of us swung down and the others staying below. Of course, it is to be hoped that prosperity in our neighbouring countries will develop in such a way that this present wage delta will be reduced. It only needs an offensive Austrian industrial policy and not any protectionist church-tower policy, as the colleague Kaufmann has indicated here. And as much as I like what Van der Bellen said, as much as I liked his arguments: You have to put that into practice. What has been played out here as the power of consumers must be brought to the streets. This is not quite so easy as long as the products do not vote. In addition – and this is very important to me, and I conclude with it –: What was done here with Semperit has tradition in the company from which it was sold out. That's not the only case. For example, I am thinking only of Ruthner, a highly innovative company that was in trouble. It was sold under price, and the buyer was able to get a multiple of the purchase price there within three years of the balance sheet. But at the same time he shut down and relocated the research, and at the same time he transferred the know-how and made the company an extended workbench. These are not new phenomena, and this is why the Federal Government is calling for it not to be so surprised, not to react in such an amazement when such a thing happens. And the trade union would also be well advised, not only if the pain occurred to call for some quick pain, but also to address the problems at an early stage. And the bank that sold it had at that time a politically clearly aegisable Director-General, who called it Androsch. It was mostly owned by the public. So if not even if the public hand is the majority owner of a bank, which also has industrial firms and participations, if not even economic policy was able to act differently, even though it had the immediate possibilities, then I hope that now that it no longer has these opportunities, because ownership has been abandoned, it is very small, if not finally an industrial policy begins in the European dimension. If this is the lesson from the case of Semperit – at least in a long-term dimension – it would be worth it. In the short term, however, the Federal Government is cordially invited to think more than beautiful words.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20121	Mr Leikam is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20143	Federal Minister! Mr President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In my contribution, I will not deal with the change of the Foreign Law, but with the border control law. With Austria's accession to the Schengen Treaty, Austria undertakes to carry out enhanced control at the EU's external borders, while at the other hand, border crossings at the internal borders are sought without the appropriate checks on persons, but this is only possible if Schengen's standard is actually reached at the EU's external borders. Austria must therefore safeguard the interests of all the Schengen Contracting States at its external borders. We have a border control law since 1969, which still has its validity, but which is no longer sufficient due to the accession of Austria to the EU. Therefore, adjustments have become necessary through a new border control law. Closely linked to this border control law, of course, is also the establishment of the border service. By joining the Schengen Treaty, we have committed ourselves to creating a standard of Schengen at our external borders. And we are in the process of finally reaching this Schengen standard. With 1. 7. In 1997, it should be possible for the 1 400 kilometres of EU external borders to actually have Schengen standards. By this date, everything that is necessary for this Austrian border service will be completed structurally. However, there are still some difficulties, and this is also to be noted, in providing the personnel to the fullest extent possible by this date. Therefore, the assistance of the Austrian federal army is urgently needed. For in the case of executives, about 1,000 planning posts are insufficient at that time to ensure full compliance with this border service according to Schengen standards. There will then be about 2 000 executive officers supported by the Austrian Federal Army, and it should therefore be possible to reach this standard as well. As regards German criticism, which was to be heard a few weeks ago, that Austrian control at the EU's external borders does not meet both human and technical requirements, it should also be said that, when we are done, the Germans are likely to have their own problems at the Bavarian-Austrian border to accommodate their staff accordingly. I would therefore like to conclude by saying: The faster we will all work together to reach the Schengen standard at the EU's external borders, the faster we will be able to give the people the necessary freedom of travel in dealing with borders.

The absolute freedom of travel within the internal borders is guaranteed by the so-called Schengen Agreement, to which Austria, after it has fulfilled the conditions next year, is also to join. Abs. 1 in this Schengen Agreement, ladies and gentlemen, reads as follows: "The internal borders may be exceeded at any place without the control of persons." This, of course, means for the participating countries that external borders must be optimally secured and controls carried out within them must be carried out accordingly.
